New Louie Barry connection should seriously excite Sheffield Wednesday fans

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A new era is underway at Sheffield Wednesday following the appointment of Dave Challinor as the club's new manager.

After a mixed start to life back in League One, Wednesday parted company with Henrik Pedersen last month, and it has not taken long for the South Yorkshire outfit to find his replacement.

It has been a frustrating few weeks for Wednesday on the pitch, with back-to-back away defeats at Notts County and Doncaster Rovers spelling the end of Pedersen's tenure before interim manager Matt Hamshaw oversaw consecutive home draws against Cambridge United and Blackpool.

However, while it was a disappointing afternoon for the Owls as they were held to a 1-1 draw by struggling Blackpool on Sunday, the headlines were instead dominated by the appointment of Challinor, who was confirmed as the club's new manager on the morning of the game.

Given his links to Wednesday's sporting director Simon Wilson, who he worked with previously at Stockport County, it had long been rumoured that Challinor would be taking over at Hillsborough at some stage following his departure from Edgeley Park in June.

Challinor spent almost five years in charge of the Hatters, during which time he led them from the National League to League One, and he missed out on promotion to the Championship in what proved to be his final game as his side were beaten 4-1 by Bolton Wanderers in the play-off final at Wembley.

The 51-year-old, who has also had spells with the likes of AFC Fylde and Hartlepool United, holds the remarkable record of finishing in the play-offs or higher in each of his 15 full seasons as a manager, and he arrives with Wednesday sitting ninth in the League One table, just two points from the top six.

It is fair to say that Challinor's appointment has been well-received by Wednesday fans, with many feeling that the seven-time promotion winner is the right man to lead the club back to the Championship.

Challinor's impressive track record will no doubt equally excite the Owls dressing room as they prepare to begin work under their new manager, but one player in particular may be thrilled about the change in the dugout.

Louie Barry worked under Challinor on three separate occasions at Stockport during his loan spells from Aston Villa, and the forward produced the best form of his career across his various stints at Edgeley Park.

Barry linked up with Challinor for the first time in the 2023-24 season as he joined the Hatters on a season-long loan, and he scored nine goals and provided four assists in 22 appearances to help the club to the League Two title.

After the success of his first loan, Barry returned to Stockport in the summer of 2024 ahead of their return to League One, and he took his form to another level in the third tier, netting 16 goals and registering three assists in 24 games before being recalled by Villa in January.

The Hatters secured a third loan deal for Barry in January, and while injury limited him to just 10 appearances in the second half of last season, he still managed to score four goals, including netting in the second leg of the play-off semi-final victory over Stevenage.

Barry made a promising start to his Wednesday career after his permanent move from Aston Villa this summer, scoring three goals and providing one assist in his first six appearances, but he was ruled out for three months after sustaining an ankle ligament injury in early September.

As he continues to make good progress in his recovery, it has been suggested that Barry could make his return earlier than expected after the November international break, meaning Challinor may not have to wait too long to have the 23-year-old at his disposal, which is a mouthwatering prospect for Owls supporters.

While Barry has started to debunk the theory that he cannot deliver outside of Stockport since his arrival at Wednesday, he did struggle to hit the same heights in his other loans away from Aston Villa, failing to register a single goal contribution in a combined total of 13 appearances during injury-hit spells with Hull City and Sheffield United in the Championship in 2025.

The Owls have won just two of their nine league games during Barry's absence, and they have only scored 11 goals in that time, with five of those coming in the victory over AFC Wimbledon last month, so the forward has certainly been missed.

Is Dave Challinor the right man for Sheffield Wednesday or should the Owls have considered other options? Tell us in the comments below!

Such was Barry's influence in the early weeks of the season, there is every chance that Pedersen may still be in a job had he not lost his main creative spark, but Wednesday supporters will be confident that Challinor can unlock more from him than their former manager would have been able to.

If Challinor can help Barry to rediscover the explosive form he produced on a regular basis at Stockport, he could be celebrating an eighth career promotion with the Owls come the end of the season.

Opinion: Future world-class defender is coming to Liverpool, he's the next Van Dijk

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There's an exciting and future world-class defender coming to Liverpool.

Julian Ward has been tasked with building a dynasty at Anfield. Just as his former colleague, Michael Edwards, has done.

The club's new sporting director is not exactly a stranger to this role. He has been here before. In 2022, Ward was appointed as Edward's successor.

Under his leadership, Liverpool extended Mo Salah's contract and brought in several key players like Luis Diaz.

But ultimately, disagreements with the coaching department took their toll on Ward. He stepped down only to return a year later when the coaching department had left.

Now with Andoni Iraola it looks like there is real synergy. The Spaniard is very open to working with any kind of player and is able to adapt to difficult challenges.

Liverpool's philosophy under Julian WardThe club's target for the next few years is clear. Liverpool want to return to the upper echelons of the footballing world. But Ward will understand that this cannot be achieved over one transfer window. Rebuilding takes time. Liverpool are only just at the beginning of the rebuild phase. But already, the club has brought in some really exciting young players. From Jeremy Jacquet to Hugo Ekitike and Victor Munoz. Not to mention how exciting Rio Ngumoha is looking right now after finally being given the freedom to unleash his flair by Iraola. There's Trey Nyoni coming through and plenty of other young Liverpool players like Joshua Abe, Erik Farkas, Luca Eden and Mor Talla Ndiaye hoping to follow suit. In the meantime, Ward is working on targeting the best young players across the globe.

Levi's stop-motion masterpiece is a lesson in Christmas advertising

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Last year, it seemed that some of the biggest brands had declared the death of the opulent, magical holiday advert. AI slop would do, they decided.

Coca-Cola's AI Christmas ad was one of the most controversial, while McDonald's pulled its festive effort due to the backlash. This year, it seems we're seeing a strong counter movement, with big brands making a point of their commitment to handcrafted art.

First, there was the glorious Prada Christmas ad, directed by former Disney animator John Pomeroy. Now Levi's has pulled out the stops for a very different animated gem, showing that it's not just luxury brands that see the value of human craftsmanship in advertising.

Levi's Let’s Get Fuzzy stop-motion holiday campaign was produced by the London-based production studio Blinkink . The animation begins as a play on horror movies, making it a fun segue from Halloween. A monster interrupts a group of friends' cosy festive retreat in a snow-covered cabin. But Fuzzyeti's discovery of festive jumpers makes him quickly succumb to the holiday spirit.

The medium of painstaking stop-motion animation was chosen to highlight Levi's heritage of craftsmanship. As shown in the making of video below, a six-person team spent more than 2,000 hours over six and a half weeks building and painting the sets, including three cabins at different scales, along with 400 miniature trees, and four faces for each character.

The clothes are miniature replicas of real Levi's products – a stylist was on hand to ensure accuracy. They were moved for every frame of the animation to make them feel life like, but requiring four hours of work of every second of animation.

A reference edit was made with actors and the directors themselves acting out the roles (including a specialist monster actor for Fuzzyyeti). This was then used as the guide for the animators.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Levi's has form in animation, from the psychedelic 2D short The Stranger in 1971 to Clayman with Shaggy’s Boombastic in claymation in 1995. Today, the decision to use such a laborious handcrafted process makes a bigger statement, creating added distinction and value amid so much AI-generated content.

Your local food truck might not worry about their ugly AI flyers if they can do the job for cheap. But for big brands, proof of work like the behind-the-scenes video above has become as important as the finished advert. Avoiding the embarrassment of an 'AI content' label isn't enough, they're taking steps to proactively signal that an advert was human made.

Hermès and Ralph Lauren are just two fashion brands that have been doing this by mentioning the people behind their ads in the captions on social media. Naming the creative team behind a campaign has become a signal of craftsmanship, and the implication is clear: human-made art is seen as a sign of a premium brand.

A post shared by Ralph Lauren (@ralphlauren)

Apple's also been on the trend with its embroidered logo and the stop-motion ad for Apple Games, which was also made by Blinklink. The resurgence of interest in stop motion is likely to continue to grow with Laika's Wildwood doing well at the box office.

Joe is a regular freelance journalist and editor at Creative Bloq. A veteran news writer and photographer, he now works as a project manager at the London and Buenos Aires-based design, production and branding agency Hermana Creatives. There he manages a team of designers, photographers and video editors who specialise in producing visual content and design assets for the hospitality sector. He also dances Argentine tango.

I finally saw the Slate Truck — 7 things that really stood out to me about this $25,000 EV

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Here at Tom’s Guide our expert editors are committed to bringing you the best news, reviews and guides to help you stay informed and ahead of the curve!

Sign up to get the latest updates on all of your favorite content! From cutting-edge tech news and the hottest streaming buzz to unbeatable deals on the best products and in-depth reviews, we’ve got you covered.

Our weekly newsletter is expertly crafted to immerse you in the world of streaming. Stay updated on the latest releases and our top recommendations across your favorite streaming platforms.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Over the past year, I’ve tested a handful of “affordable” EVs — which are, in a relative sense, priced lower than the national average of around $50,000. Depending on who you talk to, options below $40,000 look genuinely attractive, and anything under that threshold almost feels too good to be true.

Yet, that’s exactly where the Slate Truck lands with its $24,950 starting price. For an all-electric pickup, I have to admit I’m impressed by the genuine real-world capability it delivers on paper: a rear-mounted motor pushing 181 horsepower and 195 lb-ft of torque, a 65 kWh battery pack, 1,500 pounds of payload capacity, NACS DC fast-charging compatibility, and an estimated 205 miles of range.

While those specs might look modest next to six-figure behemoths like the Rivian R1T, Chevrolet Silverado EV, and GMC Sierra EV, you can't ignore just how accessible that price point is. But after getting up close with the Slate Truck in person, a few details caught me completely off guard. Here’s what stood out the most.

Prior to seeing the Slate Truck in person, I already knew it was scaling things back with crank windows and manual seats. Even in 2026, I still come across a handful of cars with manual seats — though that’s usually reserved strictly for the passenger side, and it's rarer still to find both seats manual.

So while manual adjustment wasn’t a shock, I was taken aback to find that the side mirrors are entirely manual, too. I can live with crank windows and mechanical seat levers, but manual side mirrors? That’s a tougher pill to swallow, simply because it’s been ages since I’ve had to adjust a mirror by hand.

The Slate Truck I saw featured a limited-edition wrap created in collaboration with Smarties. Yes, the candy rolls you probably remember from childhood.

Even though the "Blank Slate" base model rolls off the assembly line with a generic gray exterior, Slate sets itself apart by offering bold, fun vinyl wraps right out of the gate. It’s an ingenious way to make the truck feel uniquely yours, especially with options to match your personal style.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

I’m just curious to see how well these wraps hold up after enduring a few harsh Northeast winters.

Compact pickups are already at a disadvantage due to their smaller footprint, and in a two-seater like the Slate Truck, cabin space is especially tight. Since the seats sit flush against the barrier separating the cabin from the flatbed, there’s practically no leeway to recline them for a more relaxed driving position.

That might not be an issue if the Slate Truck is strictly kept to quick local errands, but plenty of drivers will want to take it on longer road trips. Fighting driver fatigue starts with comfortable ergonomics, so the lack of recline here makes long stretches behind the wheel tough to manage.

I’ve seen deeper, longer beds on full-size pickups, but the Slate Truck’s usable cargo space rivals compact pickups I’ve tested, like the Hyundai Santa Cruz.

Personally, I think the bed is the perfect size for everyday needs — more than spacious enough for weekly Costco runs, while still handling the occasional large piece of furniture without breaking a sweat.

For many drivers, maneuvering into a tight parking spot is an everyday source of stress. Given the Slate Truck’s aggressive cost-cutting measures, I worried that a backup camera might have been left out — but thankfully, it wasn't.

As federal regulations mandate rear-visibility systems on all new vehicles, Slate included one by mounting an integrated camera right near the tailgate latch, taking the guesswork and anxiety out of parallel parking.

Even knowing the Slate Truck favors a minimalist approach, stepping inside the cabin to find it so stripped-back is an eye-opener. It’s a refreshingly clutter-free dashboard: simple mechanical climate knobs and an integrated phone mount anchor the center dash, while slim air vents span nearly the full width of the interior.

Behind the steering wheel sits a compact digital instrument cluster displaying only essential driving telemetry. It’s an minimalist setup that cuts out distractions and keeps your focus squarely on the road.

The biggest challenge the Slate Truck faces is convincing buyers the company will actually be around for the life of the vehicle. Given its ultra-aggressive pricing, I’m genuinely surprised the EV maker is offering such a generous 10-year, 110,000-mile battery and powertrain warranty.

For a startup automaker, including this coverage at no extra charge gives budget-conscious drivers real peace of mind that a cheap entry price won’t turn into a financial money pit down the road.

Previously, he held editor roles with PhoneArena, Android Authority, Digital Trends, and SPY. Outside of tech, he enjoys producing mini documentaries and fun social clips for small businesses, enjoying the beach life at the Jersey Shore, and recently becoming a first time homeowner.

Love Is Blind Season 11: Find Out Who Broke Up and Who Got Married

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

During the Love Is Blind season 11 finale, four couples revealed whether they ready to say yes to forever after falling in love inside of the pods.

Major Warning: This article contains spoilers for the Love Is Blind season 11 finale.

Some couples were determined to hold tight to their vision. But that’s not to say the last few episodes of Love Is Blind didn’t get wicked dramatic.

As a quick recap: During the Netflix series’ 11th season—centered around singles hailing from Boston—though six pairs initially got engaged after finding one another in the pods, ultimately four couples made it all the way until their fateful weddings.

Before their big reveal, Scott “Stew” Stewart and Jacqueline “Jax” Guelli seemed to be off to a promising start—that is until their meeting proved that sometimes love may need some more time.

And though the abrupt end of their engagement may have put their status into blurry territory, all proved to be well between the two by the very end.

But not everyone was able to make amends. After Rob Feeley’s outbursts towards both production and his fellow castmates took center stage twice during episode 10, his bride-to-be, his fiancée Michaella Giorgio came to one clear realization.

"I don’t deserve any of what I’m getting," she expressed while crying to one castmate at a pod squad meet up. "I love him. It’s just a lot. And we’re not getting married. I cannot marry this man under any circumstances."

Giorgio also confirmed to her then-fiancé that while she had “genuine feelings” for him, their relationship was “obviously not gonna work out.”

As for Feeley, he later reflected on his actions, noting the passing of his father and two friends within the past year may have played a role into how he reacted both on and off camera: “I think I have more things going on than some people might have going on.”

So, by the time the big day rolled around, John Shannon and Sarah Williams, Wilson Monteiro and Wilnie Louis, Cam Halloran and Aneha Singh as well as Blake Carver and Erika Ellis were faced with the possibility of forever.

Who really said I do? Keep reading to see who made it down the aisle and into a clearer future…

Though yacht captain Blake’s romance with marketing manager Erika appeared to be smooth sailing after the pods, just one week before their wedding, the couple realized they may be in too deep due to their dramatically different lifestyles.

“I don’t want to hold you back,” Blake told Erika during the season 11 finale. “I don’t want you to be that captain’s wife on the widow walk. I don’t want to marry you and then for you to feel held hostage while I’m sailing around, gone for weeks on end. And then you’ve got a timeline with kids and everything.”

“I just think at this point, there’s too many concerns, too many questions that I can’t say yes at the altar,” she shared. “I just think the best thing to do is to leave the experiment.”

And while Erika cried over their decision, Blake had already concluded their ship had sailed.

“The experiment is done, but it’s the right move,” Blake confirmed. And when a producer asked if he ever had a love like this, he replied: “I’m not gonna answer that one…I’m feeling a little relieved, a little exhausted.”

While both Wilson and Wilnie had the times of their lives at their respective bachelor and bachelorette celebrations, the party was definitely over before they reached the altar.

In fact, they couldn’t see eye-to-eye on life's more mundane tasks, including dividing up cooking duties and household chores.

“When we got out of the pods, you got everything I promised you, plus,” Wilson told Wilnie following an off-camera disagreement, later adding, “You said it countless times, ‘I’ll do the things for you that make you happy.’ And I’ve mentioned what those things might be, but then you’ll be like, ‘But I don’t like doing that…I’m not used to doing that.’”

As for Wilnie, she couldn’t disagree more. “I don’t work in the home,” the mental health counselor explained. “I have long, emotionally draining days and whether you agree with that, when I get home, it is hard for me to always be at your level.”

Ultimately, for Wilson, there were too many issues at hand for them to tie the knot. “It’s not that you’re a bad person,” the senior compliance manager shared, “you’re a phenomenal person but maybe marriage is not for us.”

By the end, though a tearful Wilnie confirmed she still loved Wilson, she doesn’t regret their path.

Cam Halloran & Aneha Singh: To Be Determined

Once Aneha and Cam entered the real world, her hesitation to marry someone with a different background and religious beliefs —especially in the wake of her family’s disapproval—and their communication skills proved to be hurdles. And, yet, they seemed determined to scale them together.

However, once asked whether she was ready to say “I do,” the principal engineer shared that she couldn’t, telling her fiancé: “I still believe you’re my person, but I do not today. I need more time.”

As for Cam, he assured her that he completely understood. “It’s okay,” the human resource coordinator replied. “it’s a big decision. I feel like we both need to be one hundred percent confident.”

But once Aneha declared she didn’t and danced away from the altar while stressing to Cam that he was still her fiancé, he teared up watching her leave. As he later explained to his family, he simply didn’t “know what happens now.”

Shortly after, Aneha and Cam did end up meeting up one final time. And while Cam expressed that he still loved and cared for her, he needed time to process the “I don’t” of it all.

When healthcare worker Sarah started to fall for account executive John, they initially disagreed on fundamental topics such as abortion rights and vaccinations. However, despite their perspectives, they both said yes to spending their lives together, becoming the only couple to get married during the show’s 11th season.

“It’s fun looking back on the first few days ‘cause I don’t think we liked each other very much,” she shared. “And then, some people here heard the story about you maybe not being too jazzed to keep talking to me. But I love that that happened, because I think that was a catalyst for us.”

She noted, “After that, I’ve never been so sure on someone, and I had never even seen you. And then when I saw you, I felt like I’d already known you a lifetime.”

Even though the pair were shaping up to be frontrunners once they left the pods, Rob and Michaella’s relationship came to a screeching halt after he had two shocking public outbursts during episode 10.

After a hangout with his fellow friends went left once a bartender cut one of them off, Rob then came to the cast meet-up with another bone to pick.

"I’m going down in this thing as the biggest piece of s--t,” he yelled at the bar. “Right where Netflix wants me, buddy. Dude, they’re gonna manipulate this whole thing into something crazy. I don’t give a f--k."

In fact, his behavior amongst his castmates—in addition to other uncomfortable moments between the two of them—made Michaella think twice about completing the rest of their journey.

"I don’t deserve any of what I’m getting," she expressed through tears. "I love him. It’s just a lot. And we’re not getting married. I cannot marry this man under any circumstances."

Scott “Stew” Stewart & Jacqueline “Jax” Guelli: Split

Despite their initial chemistry in the pods, Stew and Jax fizzled out almost immediately once they met face-to-face.

However, even after ending their engagement, they’ve coming to a crystal-clear understanding as friends. In fact, Stew shared that even though Jax had turned down producers’ offer to meet up again immediately after their reveal, she then reached out on her own terms.

"It was super sincere," he previously told E! News. "She explained that it wasn't about me. It was about just the emotional overload of the previous couple weeks, and I think once we got settled back in Boston, once kind of normal life was allowed to resume, we were able to kind of catch our breaths."

Heading into their big day, Amber—who is mom to daughter Emma—expressed worry over Jordan actually saying “I do” when it came time.

But, her fears were put to rest as the two both ended up saying yes, leaving almost everyone present to shed tears, including her daughter.

However, their relationship came to an end just four months later, the former couple revealed during an emotional face-off at the March 2026 season 10 reunion.

Brittany Wicker & Devonta Anderson: Split

Right before their nuptials, season 10's Brittany revealed she was “shocked” when Devonta called it all off.

“Today was supposed to be our wedding day but it’s not,” she told the camera. “But right now, I feel like everything I want isn’t happening.”

As for her now ex, though Devonta noted that everything between then was “great,” ultimately, “I can’t walk into a marriage with self-doubt of ‘Can I love you how you need to be loved?’ I think that’s why I couldn’t get to the altar. I just can’t do it.”

And though Brittany walked away noting that they’re “still going to be together,” Devonta confirmed he’s not on the same page.

Indeed, Devonta revealed at the March 2026 reunion that he is now engaged to an old friend and the two are expecting a baby. The pair welcomed son Kyler Lee Anderson in August 2026.

Brianna “Breezy” McNees & Connor Spies: Split

Though season 10 star Bri decided she and Connor weren’t ready to get married just days before their wedding, at the show’s March 2026 reunion, the pair confirmed they’re still very much in love and working on eventually getting to their happily ever after.

But their journey reached a hard stop weeks later, Connor sharing on the Table for 1 podcast in June, "I'm not dating Bri anymore."

The season 10 couple's engagement came to an abrupt end once Chris confessed to Jessica that he wasn't physically attracted to her.

However, Jessica has since found love in another pod mate, as she revealed at the March 2026 reunion, with fellow doctor Haramol Gill.

"You are everything, Jess," Haramol told her during the special. "Every room you walk into gets brighter."

Though the season 10 pair nearly exchanged vows after falling in love sight unseen, their differing perspectives on having kids—Emma has voiced her uncertainty while Mike has been firm about having them—was too much of a factor to compromise on.

“I do love you,” Mike told Emma. “Right now, I’m not ready to say I do. I want kids. And I want to know that you definitely, 100 percent want a family with me. I don’t wanna say yes and jump into something and then it changes down the road.”

And though Emma noted she can “see glimpses of a family,” in their future, Mike shared that he wants to her to be certain.

"If we’re gonna get married, I want you to know that you want a family with me for sure,” he explained. “I don’t want it to be half-ass…And you have your reasons and I respect them and respect them but I just can’t say yes without knowing that. So, I’m sorry."

Mike has since hard-launched a new romance.

Before walking down the aisle in the season 10 finale, the soccer coach compared his wedding day to a “penalty shootout with everything on the line,” noting that he was confident that they would make it.

However, Ashley made it clear that it wouldn’t be the goal and jumped in when he was presented with whether he would say “I do.”

"I know what you’re going to say," she told him. "I know you don’t choose me and I’m not choosing you either.” Ashley went on to list a number of reasons why they didn’t work out, including questions about his integrity. A point Alex made sure to address in that very same moment.

"I do disagree to an extent about the integrity," he told her. "I felt as though the questions about my past were more to try and catch me in something than really understand me."

And if you’re wondering whether he would do anything differently: "Absolutely no regrets on my end, I’m so pleased and proud of myself as a person."

Victor "Vic" St. John & Christine Hamilton: Married

From the start of season 10, Christine and Vic were instantly a match made in pod heaven, with the two both happily agreeing to their future together.

"I never thought I would fall in love in such a unique experiment, and Christine,” he noted during his vows, “the moment we spoke in those pods, we just connected…You know more about me than anyone else on this planet."

She marked his 35th birthday in September with an Instagram tribute to "the kindest, most hardworking, humble, strong, gentle, passionate, joyful, God-fearing man."

In the end, Anton's drinking habits and financial situation didn't seem to match what Ali was seeking.

"I can't be your wife," she told the truck driver at the altar in season nine. "The person I fell in love with in the pods isn't the person I've gotten to experience in real life."

"It just didn't feel right or how I would expect that moment to feel like," she told the cameras. "I don't feel he was super forthcoming about his lifestyle."

Anton's reaction? "I don't know if it's looks, I don't know if it's the vibes or I don't know if it's the way that we were living together. But I can say I've done nothing but care for her."

Though the couple bonded over having loved ones with Type 1 diabetes, their differing lifestyles (he preferred a more simplistic way of life; she loves luxury) were a breaking point for Megan, who called off the wedding at the end of the season nine finale.

"We're ships in the night, like two different people," the entrepreneur told him. But the metaphor passed him by, with Jordan insisting, "I don't see it."

When Megan cited his picky eating habits and his inflexible travel schedule as a dad, Jordan told her, "I don't know why you chose me in the pods then."

"Initially I leaned into opposites attract but I still just have reservations," she explained before they each began crying. "Knowing this today, there's no way I wanted to go to the altar and put either of us through that."

Jordan shared his one regret was introducing her to his son, Luca, saying in a confessional, "I feel like she doesn't love me for me and that's the closure I need."

Megan admitted the ordeal had her "questioning if I'm even cut out to be a mom. Maybe I am too caught up in my own life."

But at the October 2025 reunion, she revealed she and partner Paul Wegman had welcomed a son they named Brooks.

Kalybriah Haskin & Edmond L. Harvey: Split

They both confessed they "love the hell out of" each other at the altar, but season nine's Kalybriah turned Edmond down with an apology.

"You deserve someone that is 100 percent at the altar and I'm not 100 percent right now," she told the realtor. Still, she stressed, "My no doesn't mean that I don't love you."

While Edmond broke down in tears, Kalybriah thanked him for being "so graceful" as she made the "hard" decision. When he asked if she'd date him "down the road," Kalybriah said she'd never write him off.

"I am shocked," he said in a confessional of her decision. "There was no bright side."

The pair didn't make it to the altar in season nine, as Joe ended the relationship after having a breakdown at his tux fitting.

"On and off camera, Joe was different," Madison later told Glamour. "Off camera, he was far more reassuring. We were very intimate, very touchy. And then on camera, it was the opposite, and you kind of see that at the pool party."

Madison even asked Joe about his attraction for her, which he confessed was an issue during their vacation.

"I kind of dug deeper, like, 'What is your typical type?' And he was like, 'I just usually date more petite women,'" she recalled to the outlet. "I am a really confident person. At that moment, it kind of put me into this insecure feeling."

Joe defended his comment, telling the outlet, “I was trying, poorly, to explain a pattern in my past dating experiences, not to compare or criticize."

Nick dumped Annie before the wedding day in season nine, explaining he was hurt that she continued to say she loved him more than he loved her.

However, the hairstylist refused to believe it was over and tried to convince Nick to continue, saying, "Just be in this and love me."

"You can't make one plus one equal three," he said, prompting her to say, "Watch me."

Nick ultimately told Annie, “The reason that I am not willing to marry you is because I am unhappy."

Lauren O'Brien and David Bettenburg: Split

Their romance failed to take off once the season eight pair landed in Honduras, with Dave revealing he and his then-fiancée had mutual friends in common. But it wasn’t the similar inner circles that proved to be the problem, but rather, Dave’s hesitation in moving forward after they told him about Lauren hooking up with someone they knew before the show.

Not to mention, Dave also didn’t want to introduce Lauren to his friends and family, noting that his dad didn’t know of his engagement and that his sister—who became a big point of contention—disapproved of their relationship altogether.

One of the most compatible couples of season eight, most viewers assumed Monica and Joey would make it down the aisle and beyond. However, when it came to saying "I do," Monica instead said, "I don’t right now."

"In this moment, it's 100 percent," she said through tears, "and I just feel like we're both, we're not quite there." As for Joey, he agreed with every word.

“That's totally okay,” he explained after hugging her. “I think we both have a tremendous amount of love for each other, and that's apparent. But I think there's bigger things and bigger growth. And I agree, I don’t think I would be ready. And to be completely honest, I’m also not 100 percent at this time.”

Though season eight's Ben and Sara seemed to disagree on major components of their life together—including religion and politics—by wedding day, the pair seemed to have gotten on the same page. But ultimately, they decided to end their shared story.

As Sara—who said, "I don’t" to a future with Ben—explained, "Ben, I love you so much but I always wanted a partner to be on the same wavelength, and so today, I can't. I'm sorry but I don't want that to be misunderstood."

She later added, "I know that the connection we have is so real and my heart is there, but when we talked about a lot of the values I hold so close to my heart, making this decision, my mind is telling me I can't."

Another season eight couple who seemed to be focused on the road ahead post-pods, Devin and Virginia, got along perfectly fine—that is, until the topic of having a prenup came up.

But it was even bigger issues that proved to be enough for Virginia, leading her to ultimately saying no to spending the rest of her life with Devin.

“I think that in a lot of our relationship, I wanted to protect Devin,” she said, through tears, after the leaving the altar. “And I don't know if that's a cultural thing, but it's always been important for me to protect him. So, there were even topics that he didn't want to talk about, like politics, there were just certain things he just didn't want me to push on. So there was just depths of our relationship that we didn't get to. We just didn't get there.”

There was at least one season eight couple who did say yes to a season of love past Christmas.

Though the pair seemed to face quite the hurdle (Taylor was convinced Daniel followed, then unfollowed her on Instagram before the show and therefore, knew what she looked like), they managed to clear that particular obstacle.

In fact, after exchanging emotional vows, the two enjoyed a beautiful wedding and celebratory reception.

"We did it," Daniel said while sitting alongisde Taylor after their nuptials, with Taylor adding, "We're so excited to start the next chapter." They marked two years of their "wild journey" with a March 2026 Instagram post.

Brittany Wisniewski and Leo Braudy: Split

Though the now-famed art dealer decided to pop the question to the Keeping Well author following his season seven love triangle with Hannah Jiles, the pair ultimately decided after their short engagement, they were better off as friends.

“The chemistry just wasn't there,” Brittany previously told People in a joint interview with Leo. “We had a talk in the beginning about how this could work and then time went on during the trip and the chemistry just wasn't there and we mutually felt that. We're very good communicators, so it was not a big ordeal. It was a very clean, respectful realization.”

Shortly after her appearance on the Netflix dating show, Brittany debuted her romance with musician Mod Sun in November 2024, but she confirmed in July 2025 that the pair had called it quits.

Monica Davis and Stephen Richardson: Split

Monica and Stephen’s relationship came to a screeching halt in season seven when she uncovered “really kinky” sext messages sent between her fiancé and another woman, causing the pair to break up shortly after they arrived home from Mexico. As for Stephen, he apologized in the moment during episode eight, but he also shared a message on social media once the episode aired.

"I hope I didn't ruin anyone's day by existing," he wrote on Instagram Stories in October 2024, "and if I did, I'm sorry you had to wake up this morning. I wish you didn't have to either. But I'll love you all the same."

Once Hannah and Nick embarked on life outside the pods, the season seven pair had their fair share of ups and downs—and disagreements—to say the least. (Let’s not forget: “I think you get treated as an equal when you star contributing like an equal.”)

And it all came to a head when Hannah decided they wouldn’t make it down the aisle. As for Nick, he called their breakup “one of the worst feelings of my life.”

Since then, Hannah has admitted the show forced her to do some self-introspection.

“I’ve learned a lot, I’ve learned there’s things I need to work on,” she said during an October 2024 episode of The Viall Files podcast. “For example, I am a very direct person. I know that but I think my delivery is rough. And I’ve learned that delivery is everything.”

Taylor Krause and Garrett Josemans: Married

A match made in science-based heaven (i.e. those serendipitous tattoos), season seven stars Taylor and Garrett clicked from the very beginning and just seemed destined to say, “I do.” Though they did hit a rough patch once Taylor found out he responded to an ex’s DM (according to Garrett, he confirmed he was engaged and shut it down), the rest is Love Is Blind history.

"I'm very grateful to have a life partner like my husband because no matter where we are in the world you make it feel like home," Taylor wrote in a November 2025 Instagram post marking their second anniversary. "I admire how much we have grown to build a life together and much of that has been because of you."

Yes, you read that right: Though Marissa and Ramses rose to become one of season seven's frontrunners of the couples who would make it, he ultimately called off their wedding after having second thoughts.

“We have a great future ahead of us, I want to marry you,” Marissa told her fiancé after a sit-down to which he responds, “I understand falling in love is a risk, I understand that.”

However, he noted their differences—which have included his stance on the military—may be too tough to work through. “After being married and seeing other marriages and hearing from other married people,” he added. “I just don’t think love alone is enough…I think there needs to be other things that are also working.”

As for Marissa, though she tried to navigate past their perspectives, once she confirmed it was over, she broke down into tears. “I don’t think you should’ve come to this experience,” she tells him. “How could I feel so sure? And you’re not? It sucks, I want you to choose me,” adding of their split, “This is the worst day of my life.”

She announced her marriage to former partner D. Johnson in May 2026.

Tyler Francis and Ashley Adionser: Divorced

Tyler and Ashley quickly became a fan-favorite couple after season seven viewers watched their relatively easy-going romance unfold. However, the couple hit a bump in the road once Tyler shared that he had three children, which Ashley referred to as his "sperm donor babies." (However, there’s online discourse over the extent of his involvement, which he has not addressed publicly).

Solana Microscope Offers Open-Source Monitoring for Complex On-Chain Operations

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. An alert is not a transaction blocker.

Operational tooling rarely generates the excitement of a new token launch, yet it can determine whether institutions trust a network enough to hold meaningful value on it. Teams want evidence that they can detect unexpected actions before the problem expands. Monitoring is one part of that process, not a substitute for preventive controls.

The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. The toolkit is designed to help operators inspect instructions, transactions and alerts while connecting observations to common monitoring workflows. It is an engineering tool, not an announcement that every Solana application now receives automatic protection against attacks. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

In a complex blockchain application, important activity can be hidden within technical transaction instructions that a general-purpose dashboard does not explain well. Monitoring tools try to decode those events and route relevant signals to people who can respond. This is useful for treasury movements, contract administration and unusual operational behaviour, but good alerts depend on choosing the right thresholds and maintaining the integrations.

The wider lesson is that on-chain transparency is not the same thing as operational comprehension. Transaction data may be public while a business still struggles to identify why an event matters or who should investigate it. A self-hostable tool can give teams more control over their monitoring stack, provided they manage uptime, access and alert delivery responsibly.

There is a meaningful gap between a fast transaction and a complete financial service. Solana infrastructure can settle digital assets, but an actual payments or securities product must also manage eligibility, legal rights, local currencies and exceptions. The best reporting follows the full workflow rather than assuming the chain’s speed is the entire product.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Solana’s Samsung Wallet rollout and user test and the latest Solana developer and payments coverage. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

An alert is not a transaction blocker. Microscope does not automatically fix a vulnerable programme, reverse an executed transfer or eliminate the need for security audits. False alarms can consume attention, while incomplete alert definitions can miss harmful activity. A monitored environment still requires tested incident procedures and clear accountability.

Distribution can be an advantage for blockchain applications, but the user experience needs to justify a change in behaviour. Developers also need monitoring and controls to support systems that operate continuously. A credible launch is one that describes available features, supported participants and how success will be measured after the initial announcement.

Watch for adoption in real applications, public documentation and integration examples showing how teams respond to alerts. The most useful evidence would involve measured detection speed and fewer unresolved incidents, rather than the number of dashboards deployed. For Solana developers, monitoring belongs alongside code review and permission design as a basic operating requirement.

Champions League’s highest-scoring teenagers: Yamal, Mbappe, Bellingham…

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Neither Lionel Messi nor Cristiano Ronaldo feature among the highest-scoring teenagers in Champions League history.

That’s a surprising truth given their superhuman career achievements and the fact that so many football stars have used Europe’s premier club competition as a springboard for their blossoming career.

If you’re good enough, you’re old enough. Here are the male players to have scored the most goals as teenagers in the Champions League.

Now recognised more for his Barclaysman status, ‘the Yak’ actually has a unique place in Champions League history as one of the competition’s most prolific teenagers.

He found form in the Champions League upon signing for Maccabi Haifa, including a penalty against Manchester United and a hat-trick against Olympiakos. It was that form which prompted a move to England with Portsmouth.

Yakubu managed five goals in just five games before leaving his teenage years.

It feels like Bellingham has now been around scoring worldies and standing with his arms out beside him in celebration for years, when in actual fact he’s 21. Twenty-one.

He played 23 times as a teenager in the competition, scoring six goals from midfield with Borussia Dortmund after moving from Birmingham. He’s now in a tussle to win the Ballon d’Or with teammate Vinicius Junior. Time flies when you’re bloody good at football.

Real Madrid’s hometown hero, it somehow goes unappreciated as to exactly how good Raul was, for so long.

He scored six times in eight Champions League games as a teenager for Real Madrid, a swift start on his way to becoming the first player to score 50 goals in the competition and make over 100 appearances.

The legendary Frenchman burst onto the scene as a teenager at Lyon and that was represented in his start in the Champions League, bagging six goals from nine games before his teen years were over, including a goal on his competition debut.

It’s taken him a while to properly receive his flowers, but all these years later, Benzema is correctly remembered as one of the greatest modern forwards, playing facilitator to Cristiano Ronaldo and Gareth Bale at Real Madrid before becoming the main man and firing them to European glory himself in 2022.

Of the five Champions Leagues he won, his individual performances to clinch the final one make it the most memorable.

Seven goals from his 18 games as a teenager in the Champions League was merely the beginning of a joyous career for Kluivert.

The most important of those seven goals as a teenager was the one that won Ajax the Champions League in 1995, firing in the winner after 85 minutes against a star-studded Milan and becoming the youngest player to score in a Champions League final in the process.

Scoring a first-half hat-trick on his Champions League debut for Red Bull Salzburg against Genk, Haaland looked like a special talent from that very first game and is yet to slow down.

He scored 10 goals as a teenager in the competition and bagged 12 goals in a single Champions League campaign as Manchester City won the trophy for the first time to complete the treble in 2022-23.

Mbappe became the second-youngest Frenchman to score in the Champions League behind Benzema when he fired in a goal in Monaco’s 5-3 defeat away to Manchester City in 2017.

That was the first of many and the beginning of an ascent to individual brilliance at PSG

The forward scored five and assisted six from 10 games as PSG made the final in 2020, before losing to Bayern Munich.

He’s not slowed down in Madrid either, although Mbappe is yet to win the Champions League.

Yamal has surpassed Mbappe to become the highest-scoring teenager in Champions League history, breaking the record in November 2026.

The 19-year-old set the new record after scoring in Barcelona’s 3-0 victory over Aston Villa, his 14th career goal in the Champions League.

Considering he doesn’t turn 20 until next summer, there is plenty of time for Yamal to stretch his lead even further.

Democrats win US House to secure control of Congress in midterms

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, giving them power to investigate President Donald Trump’s administration.

Democrats have won control of both chambers of the US Congress in the midterm elections, giving the party greater influence over legislation and oversight of President Donald Trump’s administration.

The party won House seats in districts where Trump beat his Democratic opponent by as much as 18 percentage points in 2024. Democrats also flipped at least seven Senate seats in Kansas, Iowa, Texas, Ohio, Maine, North Carolina and South Carolina, according to the Associated Press.

The result could reshape the outlook for crypto legislation and bring greater congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked digital asset businesses. Democratic committee leaders will gain more influence over which bills advance and which industry activities face investigation.

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, allowing them to issue subpoenas and investigate the Trump administration, the president’s family and their business interests, according to NBC News.

Financial markets commentary publication The Kobeissi Letter pointed to affordability as the defining election issue, citing high energy costs and renewed interest rate increases.

Gokhshtein Media founder David Gokhshtein blamed Republican candidates for failing to connect with voters.

“Well, Republicans got their asses handed to them. I thought they’d do better. I was wrong,” he wrote on X Wednesday.

Prediction markets had favored a Democratic sweep. Traders on Kalshi and Polymarket put the probability of Democrats winning both chambers at roughly 75% on Tuesday, before the results were announced.

Gold, silver and precious metals prices jump, dollar dips as Democrats take control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Precious metals saw strong gains on Tuesday morning, while the value of the dollar fell, following the U.S. midterm elections.

Spot gold was 2.64% higher at $4,031.98 an ounce by 3:37 a.m. ET, while spot silver jumped 4.17% to trade at $51.8495 per ounce.

Front-month gold futures added 2.53% to trade at $4,039.90, while silver futures were up 4.21% at $51.85.

Other metals seeing notable gains on Wednesday morning included platinum, which jumped 2.82% to trade at $1,455.96, and palladium, which was 3.8% higher at $969.33.

The moves came as vote counting continued in the midterms, with the Democrats projected to have won control of the House and multiple governors' offices also turning blue.

U.S. stock futures broadly fell as the results came in, while Treasury yields also moved lower. Oil prices were marginally higher, while the U.S. dollar index shed around 0.3%.

Rhona O'Connell, head of market analysis for EMEA and Asia at StoneX, said in a note on Tuesday that if the Democrats won dual Congressional control, gold should find support as rates and the dollar would likely come under pressure.

She said it would result in "fiscal brinkmanship, institutional friction, both leading to uncertainty," adding that "the Iran war and a weaker dollar would compete with [the] impact of [a] hawkish Fed."

"Less fiscal stimulus argues for lower long yields and a softer dollar, offset by debt-limit premia in bills and a Fed leaning towards another hike," she said.

Both gold and silver enjoyed record-breaking rallies in 2025, surging 66% and 135% respectively over the course of the year as investors sought safe haven assets amid geopolitical uncertainty and broader market volatility.

But the momentum has come undone this year, with rising inflation, surging bond yields and hawkish central banks removing support from the metals. Gold is down more than 7% year-to-date, while silver has tumbled almost 28%.

Spot gold rose on Monday as oil prices retreated, and is now on course for a weekly rise of around 3.7%.

Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, said in a Tuesday note that the recovery remains fragile.

"The challenge for precious metals is that geopolitical uncertainty is not automatically translating into safe-haven demand when investors are also concerned about restrictive monetary policy," she said. "They also face the risk of liquidation during sharp equity selloffs, as investors sell profitable or liquid positions to raise cash."

Bitcoin is climbing on Wednesday as the end of the US midterm elections removes a major source of uncertainty. BTC gained 1.8% to about $77,920 in early US hours, bouncing back from its lowest level in more than a month, hit in the previous session. During the vote count, the price briefly slid toward $75,700 before recovering to around $77,600. Traders stepped in to buy after weeks of steep losses, helped by US stock futures and Asian markets recovering from early declines, especially in tech. Altcoins edged up as well, with Cardano rising 1.4% and Solana, BNB and Dogecoin each gaining about 0.8%. A Democratic-controlled Congress could make crypto regulation tougher, as most pro-crypto laws of the past two years had Republican backing.

‘Painful’ – Alexis Mac Allister doesn’t hold back about ‘unpleasant situation’ at Liverpool

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Alexis Mac Allister has spoken out about his ‘painful, unpleasant situation’ at Liverpool in terms of not being offered a new contract.

The Argentine caused a stir in September when he said that he was ‘very sad‘ not to have been offered a new deal by the club, having seen fellow 2023 signings Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai both pen contract extensions earlier this year.

The Telegraph’s Dominic King reported last month that Anfield chiefs aren’t in ‘any rush’ to begin talks over a new deal for the Reds’ number 10 (who’s contracted to June 2028), despite his resurgence in form since the start of this season.

Appearing on the Los Edul show in his homeland, Mac Allister spoke candidly about Liverpool’s decision not to offer him new terms over the past few months.

The 27-year-old said: “We’re in a situation where I have a year and a half left on my contract. Last season I was told that the club’s idea was not to renew it, at least for now, and obviously that was a painful, unpleasant situation.”

When asked if the situation was making him despondent, he replied: “Not that much, because at the end of the day, I still had – and still have – time left at this club, which is what I want most – to stay here.

“The reality is that the manager has now changed, the sporting director has changed, and new investors are coming into the club, so I think I’m now in a position where anything can happen. We’ll see what happens in the future. I’ve always said that I’m very happy here, that I really enjoy it, and we’ll see.”

Mac Allister added: “Look at Szoboszlai and Gravenberch. They’re two players who were in the same contractual situation as me, and when I was told that, they renewed their contracts.

“It was more a position taken by the club, and generally that’s how things are done. At the end of the day, as a club you don’t want to lose a lot of money, and you don’t want players leaving on free transfers either.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

That the Argentine has spoken out publicly about his contract situation once more gives a strong hint that privately he’s seething at not being offered a new deal in the same way that Szoboszlai and Gravenberch were.

Admittedly Mac Allister’s collapse in form last season may have triggered a reluctance from the club to commit to a contract extension for a player who’ll be 29 when his current terms expire, and he’s a bit older than his aforementioned midfield teammates.

However, the ex-Brighton man has been excellent in the first two months of this campaign, almost as though he’s motivated by a burning desire to earn a new deal at Anfield and show that the hierarchy have been mistaken in their reluctance to tie him down for the long-term.

The 2022 World Cup winner is currently on £180,000 per week at Liverpool, a figure exceeded by 10 of his teammates (Capology), and it could be the case that he’s seeking a higher amount than what the club are prepared to offer him.

Mac Allister could hardly have made his desire for a new contract any clearer. We don’t know exactly why his situation is taking longer to resolve than those of Szoboszlai and Gravenberch, but if he keeps performing on the pitch, then surely it’s just a matter of when and not if he’s offered an extension by the Anfield hierarchy.

Manchester United in agreement over Michael Carrick sack decision after Roma win

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick has eased pressure on his job with Manchester United ‘s come-from-behind win over Roma, but the threat of the sack remains alive.

The under-pressure manager ended a run of three straight defeats - and nine games without a win - as Matheus Cunha scored twice, and was sent off, late on to down an under-strength Roma side.

Roma had raced into a 2-0 lead inside 17 minutes thanks an early Matias Soule strike and a Donyell Malen penalty. But Bruno Fernandes halved the deficit shortly after half-time before Cunha’s late double.

Carrick will now hope his team have momentum ahead of a crucial clash with Aston Villa on Sunday before the international break. The head coach will now that he is not yet out of the woods.

United's hierarchy held crisis talks over the head coach's position after a 3-0 defeat home defeat to the hands Bournemouth earlier in October.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were involved in discussions at Carrington the following days, with replacements reportedly considered.

Aston Villa boss Unai Emery, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann, fresh from leaving Germany, are among the names who continue to be linked with the vacancy. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned as a possible option.

However, United opted against making an immediate change last month, allowing Carrick to remain in charge for defeat at Chelsea and Tuesday’s late triumph over Roma.

According to The Athletic, Man Utd chiefs last week agreed to keep Carrick in post for the visit of Roma and Sunday’s visit of Aston Villa in the Premier League before reassessing the situation during the break.

That stance is not expected to change despite a much-needed victory.

Man Utd chiefs are said to be happy for Carrick to remain in charge until the international break, at which point a new manager could be appointment and given extra time to settle in the job before domestic football return.

Liverpool are reportedly interested in signing an exciting playmaker who could replace Florian Wirtz.

A rebuild is always going to have teething problems. That's what Liverpool are going through at the moment. There are numerous little areas of concern in the squad that the club's new sporting director Julian Ward is going to have to address.

Under these circumstances, while balancing an imperfect squad, Andoni Iraola has done a stellar job at Anfield.

The Spaniard has come in, embraced the culture of Liverpool and imbued his tactical style and philosophy very quickly into the core of the Reds.

It's a style that, conversely to Arne Slot's, is entertaining and has made Anfield exciting to attend for fans looking to express their love for the club.

Andoni Iraola's biggest task at LiverpoolIraola's first mission was to restore that entertainment factor to the way Liverpool play. As a club, you have to attract the fans. But beyond the first problem, Iraola's biggest task at Anfield now remains solving the Florian Wirtz mystery. The German has been a real conundrum for both Slot and Iraola. It's hard to figure out what is going wrong for a player for whom Liverpool invested so much money on. Wirtz has some flashes of brilliance, but he is nowhere near the player Liverpool thought they had signed. Iraola is going to have to get the best out of him. Every big action movie streaming this November, ranked by Rotten Tomatoes score

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Skip the scrolling and get to streaming high-octane joy rides

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Whether you are dodging holiday stress or settling in for a cozy evening on the couch, losing yourself in the high-octane spectacle of an action movie is a great way to wile away an hour or two. Thankfully, the best streaming services just added hundreds of movies on the first of November, including a ton of action movies to get your adrenaline pumping.

To help you skip the endless scrolling for what to watch and jump straight to the best stunts, car chases, and gunfights, I've tracked down every major action movie that just hit Netflix, Prime Video, HBO Max, and other major streaming platforms and ranked them from best to worst according to their critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Here is what's truly Certified Fresh — and what you may want to skip. Though who amongst us hasn't indulged in revisiting a bad movie from time to time?

Directed by Rian Johnson and with some incredible performances from Joseph Gordon-Levitt and Bruce Willis, "Looper" is a clever sci-fi thriller that combines time travel, killer action sequences and moral quandaries into one thrilling masterpiece. It's a standout in a genre with more than its fair share of duds, a thought-provoking take on a familiar concept that doesn't skimp on the high-octane set pieces.

"Looper" takes us forward to 2074, imagining a world where time travel is invented, but made illegal, and quickly taken up by the mob, who send their targets back in time to be taken out by a "Looper" (aka hitman). Gordon-Levitt's Joe is riding high atop the Looper ranks when his older self, played by Bruce Willis, comes to cut his career short — setting up an epic battle where the past and future collide.

I watched "Black Bag" after my colleague Malcolm described it as a cross between "Ocean’s Eleven" and "Mr. and Mrs. Smith," and I couldn’t agree more. Tightly crafted, smart, and with plenty of dark humor to spare, the film stars Michael Fassbender and Cate Blanchett as a married couple who are both spies for the British intelligence service.

Sign up to the weekly Tom's Streaming Guide to stay in the loop with the latest releases and top recommendations across your favorite streaming services. We’ve got you covered with a complete guide on what’s worth watching.

Fassbender’s George is a human lie detector, but when his wife's suspected of being a potential double agent, he’s forced to use his skills on the one person he thought he knew best. "Black Bag" is a sleek and satisfying thriller, and clocking in at just 94 minutes, it nails that ever-escalating sense of tension you're looking for in an action movie without overstaying its welcome.

If you never got around to checking out the rebooted "Planet of the Apes" franchise, all four movies are now on HBO Max this month. As far as reboots go, it's among the stronger ones, with CGI that holds up surprisingly well even now. The first entry, "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" follows Will Rodman (James Franco), who is testing a cure for Alzheimer's disease on chimpanzees, which dramatically increases their intelligence.

One of these chimpanzees, named Caesar (portrayed by Andy Serkis of "Lord of the Rings" fame), becomes smart enough to recognize the injustices faced by his kind. After being placed in an abusive primate shelter, Caesar leads a rebellion of genetically enhanced apes, sparking a war between humans and apes that doesn't go too well for us. If you don't mind jumping ahead (each story is pretty self-contained), 2024's "Kingdom of the Planet of the Apes" is the latest entry.

Watch "Rise of the Planet of the Apes" on HBO Max now (along with its three sequels)

Vietnam War movies are a dime a dozen, but very few can boast a perfect 100% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes. "Hamburger Hill" can, and once you watch it, you'll understand why. Set during the last stages of the Vietnam War, "Hamburger Hill" tells the story of a unit of the Army’s 101st Airborne Division led by Lt. Frantz (Dylan McDermott) that, in May 1969, spent 10 days trying to take a hill held by North Vietnamese soldiers.

Five fresh-faced recruits, Beletsky (Tim Quill), Languilli (Anthony Barrile), Washburn (Don Cheadle), Bienstock (Tommy Swerdlow), and Galvan (M.A. Nickles), join war-weary soldiers in an uphill battle that epitomizes the visceral horrors that defined the Vietnam War. They ultimately managed to win the day, but in a pyrrhic victory that's still considered to be a military disaster by most historians.

“Wargames” is a sci-fi techno-thriller that's only gotten more relevant over the years. A sleeper hit when it debuted in the '80s, it became a cult classic and even influenced real-world discussions on cybersecurity. It was also one of Hollywood's best early depictions of hacking that was both thrilling and eerily plausible.

Matthew Broderick stars as David Lightman, a teenage computer whiz who accidentally hacks into a top-secret U.S. military supercomputer, thinking Global Thermonuclear War sounds like a fun new computer game. He runs a simulation of what a nuclear war between the U.S. and the Soviet Union would look like — one that the computer, known as WOPR, begins to take seriously.

Could Buying This 1 Cryptocurrency Make You Rich?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Venice Token has skyrocketed in value this year. But can it continue its momentum over the long haul?

For the year, Venice Token (VVV +17.41%) is up a heart-pounding 1,250%. That makes it the top-performing cryptocurrency with a market cap of $1 billion or higher.

For good reason, then, some investors have embraced Venice Token as a potential millionaire-maker and a pathway to life-changing wealth. But is that really the case?

Artificial intelligence continues to be one of the few bright spots in the crypto market this year. Traditional market leaders Bitcoin and Ethereum are down 12% and 22%, respectively, for the year. Meanwhile, AI cryptos continue to soar much higher.

Of all the top AI crypto stars, Venice Token has burned the brightest, with its 1,250% return. But don't forget about Bittensor or NEAR Protocol, the latter of which is up 133% for the year. Both also represent ways to get exposure to AI via blockchain technology.

But investors have latched on to Venice Token specifically because it powers a privacy-focused version of ChatGPT. In other words, you can type in a quick prompt on the Venice AI platform and get a response in the form of text, images, or video. Best of all, your usage of the platform remains anonymous and private, so you never have to worry about large language models training on your personal data in the future.

But here's the thing: You can't simultaneously believe in companies such as Anthropic or OpenAI and Venice at the same time. Their vision of AI is very different. Anthropic and OpenAI are pushing a vision of centralized AI, in which the majority of power and control lies within the corporation. Venice is pushing a vision of decentralized, privacy-focused AI built with blockchain technology.

And that's where things get tricky for investors. The thinking until recently has been that the upcoming Anthropic and OpenAI IPOs will lift all AI boats. And that includes AI cryptocurrencies such as Venice Token.

According to this way of thinking, even more money will flood into the AI crypto space after the IPOs, helping to push up the price of Venice Token. That means crypto investors might be able to squeeze another year of triple- or even quadruple-digit returns out of Venice.

But I don't think so. The AI crypto space is littered with big names that soar in value for one year before ultimately collapsing. Several prominent early AI crypto names have already fallen by the wayside. Even AI cryptos specifically pegged to the AI agent investment thesis have already imploded.

So what makes investors think Venice Token will be any different? It's still too early to pick winners and losers in the AI space. That makes it problematic to pick cryptocurrencies you can buy and hold for the long haul. Venice makes for an interesting short-term momentum play, but is simply too risky and volatile as a long-term wealth creation tool.

Zcash Is Getting a Major Upgrade in November. Could This Be a Catalyst for Further ZEC Price Gains?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The red-hot cryptocurrency has a significant upgrade just days away.

Zcash (ZEC +14.00%), the largest privacy coin by market cap, is about to get a major technical upgrade around Nov. 5. The NU7 network upgrade will cut the time between blocks of transactions from 75 seconds to 25, making confirmations roughly three times faster.

Despite a rough year for the crypto market, Zcash has exploded in value, gaining 134% year to date. Let's take a closer look at the NU7 upgrade to see what it changes and whether it could drive further growth.

The main headline of the NU7 upgrade is the change in block time. Like other cryptocurrencies, Zcash records transactions on a blockchain, with each block containing groups of transactions. Blocks currently have a target confirmation time of 75 seconds, which will drop to 25 seconds after the upgrade.

This doesn't mean transactions go through in 25 seconds. Each exchange sets a required number of confirmations to fully verify a transaction, based on its risk tolerance. If an exchange waits for three confirmations, then transaction times on its platform would drop from nearly four minutes (225 seconds) to 75 seconds.

NU7 also changes Zcash's fee allocation. Starting at activation, 60% of each block's transaction fees are removed from circulation and placed in the network sustainability mechanism, a reserve that provides a security budget for the network. Miners keep the other 40% of transaction fees. Originally, 100% of block fees went to miners. Like Bitcoin, Zcash has a hard cap of 21 million coins, so the reserve prepares for a day when fees pay miners rather than newly issued coins.

In addition, the upgrade adds per-block limits on shielded transactions and shuts down Sprout, the blockchain's original privacy pool. Funds must be transferred out of Sprout before activation, or they'll become unusable.

Execution issues are the primary risk for the NU7 upgrade, especially since it's being activated on a faster schedule than usual. Zcash's rules normally call for activation to occur approximately three months after release of the first software for the new network. This provides exchanges, node operators, and miners with ample time to update their software before it expires.

NU7's deployment document waived the three-month requirement. A release candidate started running on Zcash's test network early last month, but the stable software for the main network only launched on Oct. 22. Anyone using outdated software could end up on a different chain.

The closure of the Sprout pool also presents potential issues. Any funds left there will be lost forever, and moving funds off that pool could temporarily expose transaction amounts.

NU7 is an important upgrade to the Zcash network, both shortening block times and setting aside fee revenue in a reserve. But I wouldn't expect it to have a big impact on the privacy coin's price.

Blockchain upgrades aren't surprises. They're scheduled in advance, giving traders time to buy in and often turning the actual activation dates into "sell-the-news" events. Zcash has also already been one of the most successful crypto investments this year, so an upgrade may not be enough to move the needle at this point.

For investors, it's still worth watching how activation goes. A clean switch to NU7 would be a positive sign, while a delay or glitch could weigh on sentiment. Either way, the upgrade's value is more likely to show up in how Zcash works than in its price.

Lyle Daly has positions in Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

PSG: Mika Godts’ time has finally arrived!

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With the injuries to Ousmane Dembélé and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, all eyes are naturally turning to Désiré Doué, expected to take more responsibility in the Parisian attack. But another player also had a good card to play: Mika Godts. Recruited this summer to replace, on paper, Bradley Barcola, the Belgian winger has so far struggled to convince. His entries into the game had often left a taste of unfinished business, to the point that the possibility of an offensive reinforcement was already mentioned to compensate for the absence of Kvaratskhelia. As if we still doubted the ability of his recruit to meet expectations. Facing Olympique Lyonnais this Monday evening, Godts therefore had an ideal opportunity to silence the first criticisms.

Still replacing at kick-off, the former Ajax player was launched while PSG came up against a particularly compact Lyon block. His first minutes, however, long resembled his previous appearances. Available, multiplying calls in depth, the Belgian was rarely served by his partners. But he never gave up, displaying constant involvement, including in defensive tasks. He notably stood out with a magnificent save on his line to prevent OL from scoring. The fact remains that Godts was expected offensively. And this time, he responded. First a decisive passer for Senny Mayulu on the opening score in Paris, he then scored his first goal in the colors of PSG with a clear right-footed strike which left Dominik Greif no chance.

A liberating performance for the Belgian winger, who did not hide his satisfaction in the mixed zone after the match. “It’s good to have scored and helped the team. I just wanted to help the team. What if I was waiting for this goal? Even before that, I am calm. I try to work every day. And when the coach needs me, I’m there to help the team. I’m happy, when you’re a striker you want to score and help with goals.”he explained. Happy to have finally unlocked his score in his new club, Godts also wanted to emphasize the importance of those around him in his adaptation. “It’s good to score here, I’m happy. I feel good in the team, they help me on and off the pitch. When I scored, I thought of my family, my wife, they are important to me and it is thanks to them that I can work hard. »

For his part, Senny Mayulu did not fail to praise the performance of his teammate, with whom he combined perfectly to open the score. “I’m happy for him, we have a good connection. But it’s with everyone, we’re a family. So I’m happy that Godts passed me and scored, it’ll do him good.”confided the young Parisian midfielder in the mixed zone. An evening which could mark a turning point in Mika Godts’ Parisian adventure. Until now struggling to find his place in the offensive rotation, the Belgian finally showed what he could bring to Luis Enrique’s collective. While PSG must deal with several major absences in attack, this performance comes at the best time. It is now up to him to confirm to prove that he can become a real offensive solution for the coming weeks.
